ISSUE #316: [P2][ui/interaction] Zoom-to-layer/first-layer framing feeds layer extents to the canvas without CRS reprojection
Labels: ['bug']
Body:
## Summary
Zoom-to-layer/first-layer framing feeds layer extents to the canvas without CRS reprojection. Specifically, Layer extents are fed to QgsMapCanvas::setExtent without CRS reprojection — zoom-to-layer and first-layer framing put the view in the wrong place whenever layer CRS != project CRS.

## Area
GUI / Application Shell / Dialogs

## Type
ui/interaction

## Severity
P2

## Baseline
- Repository: WindWang2/exp-rs
- Branch: master
- Commit: d9c59e5b8290a74cbfe6c8c9f3c7209c6cdab7dc

## Affected code
- `src/app/active_view_host.cpp:261-263, 320-322, 477-488; src/app/main_window_view.cpp:434-442` (ActiveViewHost::displayAsset / ActiveViewHost::openSource / ActiveViewHost::zoomToLayer / QgisDesktopWindow::zoomToLayer)

## User impact
- Blank or misplaced map view after loading CRS-mismatched data — the most common remote-sensing workflow (mixed CRS scenes). User must manually zoom-to-full-extent to recover; first-run experience appears broken

## Reproduction
- **Trigger (SHELLA-1)**: Load a raster whose CRS differs from the project CRS (e.g. UTM GeoTIFF into the default 4326/3857 project, or open a saved project with another CRS and add a new layer), then use Zoom to Layer, Zoom to Native Resolution, or add the first layer to an empty view

## Expected behavior
- **Expected (SHELLA-1)**: Extent is transformed layer-CRS -> canvas destinationCrs (QgsCoordinateTransform with project transform context, as QGIS does) before setExtent

## Actual behavior
- **Actual (SHELLA-1)**: Raw layer-CRS numbers are used as destination-CRS coordinates; e.g. a UTM layer's 300000..700000 m range is interpreted as degrees -> canvas frames a bogus extent tens of degrees away, rendering a blank/garbage view. First-layer framing (openSource/displayAsset) and the layer-menu/context-menu Zoom to Layer are all affected

## Root cause
- **SHELLA-1**: CONFIRMED
  - *Callchain*: `layer-tree ctx menu Zoom to Layer -> LayerTreeMenuProvider -> ActiveViewHost::zoomToLayer -> QgsMapCanvas::setExtent(layer->extent()); or file open -> QgisDesktopWindow::addRasterLayer -> ActiveViewHost::openRasterPath -> openSource -> targetCanvas->setExtent(layer->extent()) when canvas empty; or View menu 缩放到图层 -> QgisDesktopWindow::zoomToLayer`
  - *Code context*: `active_view_host.cpp:262 `targetCanvas->setExtent( layer->extent() );` (displayAsset), :322 same in openSource, :486 `m_mapCanvas->setExtent( target->extent() );` (zoomToLayer); main_window_view.cpp:438 `m_mapCanvas->setExtent(selected.first()->extent());`. QgsMapLayer::extent() is in the LAYER CRS; QgsMapCanvas::setExtent expects destination(project)-CRS coordinates (QGIS's own QgisApp::zoomToLayerExtent wraps it in QgsCoordinateTransform). No QgsCoordinateTransform exists anywhere in these two files (grep QgsCoordinateTransform src/app/active_view_host.cpp main_window_view.cpp = 0 hits). zoomToNativeResolution (active_view_host.cpp:490-515) additionally multiplies rasterUnitsPerPixel (layer-CRS units) by canvas pixel size and setExtent()s the result — same CRS class error.`

## Evidence
- **Skeptic verification**: QgsMapLayer::extent() is in layer CRS; QgsMapCanvas::setExtent -> QgsMapSettings::setExtent stores the rectangle in destination/output CRS. Reference QGIS confirms the transform step: refs/qgis/src/gui/layertree/qgslayertreeviewdefaultactions.cpp:438 'layerExtent = canvas->mapSettings().layerExtentToOutputExtent( layer, layerExtent );' before combining and zooming. No QgsCoordinateTransform / layerExtentToOutputExtent in active_view_host.cpp or main_window_view.cpp (grep = 0). Destination CRS tracks the project CRS (main_window_processing.cpp:533-534, main_window_connections.cpp:357-361 onCrsChanged), so any layer whose CRS != project CRS gets its raw extent interpreted as destination coordinates (UTM metres read as degrees -> bogus framing). No lower-layer guard exists (display manager adds layers with their own CRS).
- **Review note**: None (all four cited sites verified: active_view_host.cpp:262, 322, 486, main_window_view.cpp:438; zoomToNativeResolution 490-515 also reuses layer-CRS extents).
- **Finding SHELLA-1**: `active_view_host.cpp:262 `targetCanvas->setExtent( layer->extent() );` (displayAsset), :322 same in openSource, :486 `m_mapCanvas->setExtent( target->extent() );` (zoomToLayer); main_window_view.cpp:438 `m_mapCanvas->setExtent(selected.first()->extent());`. QgsMapLayer::extent() is in the LAYER CRS; QgsMapCanvas::setExtent expects destination(project)-CRS coordinates (QGIS's own QgisApp::zoomToLayerExtent wraps it in QgsCoordinateTransform). No QgsCoordinateTransform exists anywhere in these two files (grep QgsCoordinateTransform src/app/active_view_host.cpp main_window_view.cpp = 0 hits). zoomToNativeResolution (active_view_host.cpp:490-515) additionally multiplies rasterUnitsPerPixel (layer-CRS units) by canvas pixel size and setExtent()s the result — same CRS class error.`

## Suggested fix
- Ensure widgets are fully initialized or stubbed safely, signals/slots are correctly connected to backend results, and UI elements do not block the main event loop.

## Acceptance criteria
- Defect resolved for all members (SHELLA-1).
- CMake build and Catch2 unit test suite pass with 100% success.
- No regression in existing functionality or contracts.

## Regression tests
- Add targeted unit/integration tests covering the affected code path(s).

## Risk / compatibility
- Low risk when applying surgical, isolated fixes matching existing conventions.

## References / Dedupe
- #236 (CLOSED) covered which canvas receives first-layer setExtent (87575ea7ec targeted targetCanvas rerouting without adding a transform); the CRS-transform gap in these files is new. #195/#194 cover operator-layer CRS issues, not canvas extent framing.
- Finding SHELLA-1: #236 fixed only WHICH canvas receives setExtent (secondary-view first-layer framing) — commit 87575ea7ec diff shows targetCanvas rerouting with no transform added. Not #195/#194 (operator-layer CRS issues). No open/closed issue covers canvas extent CRS transform in these files

ISSUE #318: [P2][data/crs-nodata-metadata] MeasureTool caches QgsDistanceArea CRS/ellipsoid at construction - measurements wrong after project CRS change
Labels: ['bug']
Body:
## Summary
MeasureTool caches QgsDistanceArea CRS/ellipsoid at construction - measurements wrong after project CRS change. Specifically, MeasureTool caches QgsDistanceArea source CRS/ellipsoid once at startup — every later measurement is computed in the wrong CRS after a project CRS change or project open.

## Area
Data Model / Raster Grids / NoData & CRS

## Type
data/crs-nodata-metadata

## Severity
P2

## Baseline
- Repository: WindWang2/exp-rs
- Branch: master
- Commit: d9c59e5b8290a74cbfe6c8c9f3c7209c6cdab7dc

## Affected code
- `src/app/map_tools/measure_tool.cpp:15-34, 128-142` (MeasureTool::MeasureTool / MeasureTool::finishMeasurement)

## User impact
- Silently wrong measurement numbers for every measurement taken after any CRS change/project load — quantitative results a remote-sensing user relies on are garbage with no error signal

## Reproduction
- **Trigger (SHELLA-4)**: Start the app (project CRS = startup default), then open a saved .qgs whose CRS is different or change 工程 CRS via the status-bar selector, then measure any distance/area

## Expected behavior
- **Expected (SHELLA-4)**: Distance/area computed with sourceCrs = current canvas destinationCrs (QGIS's measure tool reconfigures QgsDistanceArea on crs changes), giving geodesic values for the visible map

## Actual behavior
- **Actual (SHELLA-4)**: The stale startup CRS is used; e.g. UTM (metre) coordinates interpreted as the startup 4326 CRS produce wildly wrong ellipsoidal lengths; unit label (mDistanceArea.lengthUnits()) also reflects the stale CRS

## Root cause
- **SHELLA-4**: CONFIRMED
  - *Callchain*: `视图->测距/测面 -> MeasureTool (constructed at app start) -> clicks append map-CRS points -> finishMeasurement -> QgsDistanceArea::measureLine (sourceCrs = stale startup CRS)`
  - *Code context*: `measure_tool.cpp:31-33 (constructor): `QgsProject *project = QgsProject::instance(); mDistanceArea.setSourceCrs( project->crs(), project->transformContext() ); mDistanceArea.setEllipsoid( project->ellipsoid() );` — the tools are constructed exactly once in MapToolManager::setupTools() during app startup (map_tool_manager.cpp:109-110) and nothing ever reconfigures mDistanceArea (no connection to QgsProject::crsChanged anywhere in the file; QgsProject::read() and QgisDesktopWindow::onCrsChanged both change project CRS without touching the tool). finishMeasurement then calls `mDistanceArea.measureLine( mPoints )` / `measurePolygon( mPoints )` where mPoints are map (canvas destination CRS) coordinates. If destinationCrs differs from the CRS captured at startup, coordinates are interpreted in the wrong CRS.`

## Evidence
- **Skeptic verification**: MeasureTool ctor sets mDistanceArea.setSourceCrs(project->crs()) and setEllipsoid once. mPoints are canvas/click coordinates in the canvas destination CRS; QgsDistanceArea::measureLine/measurePolygon interpret them in the cached source CRS. Nothing reconfigures the tool on project CRS change: no crsChanged connection in measure_tool.cpp/map_tool_manager.cpp (grep only the two ctor sets); the CRS-change paths (onCrsChanged main_window_connections.cpp:357-361 sets project CRS + canvas destinationCrs + refresh; setProjectCrs main_window_processing.cpp:533-534) never touch MeasureTool. After a CRS change or project open with a different CRS, every subsequent measurement uses the stale startup CRS. QGIS reference behavior (qgsmeasuretool re-wires QgsDistanceArea on crs changes) is absent here.
- **Review note**: None (lines 15-34, 128-142 accurate; the two tools are constructed once each in MapToolManager::setupTools(), main_window.cpp:337).
- **Finding SHELLA-4**: `measure_tool.cpp:31-33 (constructor): `QgsProject *project = QgsProject::instance(); mDistanceArea.setSourceCrs( project->crs(), project->transformContext() ); mDistanceArea.setEllipsoid( project->ellipsoid() );` — the tools are constructed exactly once in MapToolManager::setupTools() during app startup (map_tool_manager.cpp:109-110) and nothing ever reconfigures mDistanceArea (no connection to QgsProject::crsChanged anywhere in the file; QgsProject::read() and QgisDesktopWindow::onCrsChanged both change project CRS without touching the tool). finishMeasurement then calls `mDistanceArea.measureLine( mPoints )` / `measurePolygon( mPoints )` where mPoints are map (canvas destination CRS) coordinates. If destinationCrs differs from the CRS captured at startup, coordinates are interpreted in the wrong CRS.`

## Suggested fix
- Validate grid compatibility rigorously, propagate NoData masks across all processing steps, and preserve CRS/metadata on output datasets.

## Acceptance criteria
- Defect resolved for all members (SHELLA-4).
- CMake build and Catch2 unit test suite pass with 100% success.
- No regression in existing functionality or contracts.

## Regression tests
- Add targeted unit/integration tests covering the affected code path(s).

## Risk / compatibility
- Low risk when applying surgical, isolated fixes matching existing conventions.

## References / Dedupe
- No closed issue mentions MeasureTool or QgsDistanceArea (#214 was the ROI spectrum GDAL scan — different file/symptom). Not covered.
- Finding SHELLA-4: No closed issue mentions MeasureTool or QgsDistanceArea (#214 was ROI spectrum GDAL scans, different file/symptom). Verified mDistanceArea is never re-set at HEAD

ISSUE #320: [P2][ui/interaction] Ribbon Workflow tab renders empty (layout() on QScrollArea is null) - #215 fixed signals sit in unreachable branches
Labels: ['bug']
Body:
## Summary
Ribbon Workflow tab renders empty (layout() on QScrollArea is null) - #215 fixed signals sit in unreachable branches. Specifically, Ribbon 流程 (Workflow) tab renders empty — pageW->layout() on a QScrollArea is always null, so all five workflow buttons are never created and the closed #215 fix lives in unreachable dead code.

## Area
GUI / Application Shell / Dialogs

## Type
ui/interaction

## Severity
P2

## Baseline
- Repository: WindWang2/exp-rs
- Branch: master
- Commit: d9c59e5b8290a74cbfe6c8c9f3c7209c6cdab7dc

## Affected code
- `src/app/shell/ribbon_controller.cpp:1024-1084 (key line 1026); makeTabPage 151-175; pageLayoutOf 177-187` (RibbonController::createRibbonBar / RibbonController::makeTabPage)

## User impact
- Whole feature surface of the workflow ribbon tab is dead; #215 is effectively unresolved at HEAD despite being closed; users can only run/stop workflows from the pipeline editor dock toolbar

## Reproduction
- **Trigger (SHELLB-1)**: Open the app, click the 流程 tab in the ribbon

## Expected behavior
- **Expected (SHELLB-1)**: Two groups (流程编辑 / 流程控制) with five large buttons as coded

## Actual behavior
- **Actual (SHELLB-1)**: Blank tab page; workflow run/stop controls missing from the ribbon

## Root cause
- **SHELLB-1**: CONFIRMED
  - *Callchain*: `user clicks 流程 tab (stack index created by addTab at line 1083) -> QStackedWidget shows the empty QScrollArea page; no button exists, so PipelineEditorDock::runFullWorkflowRequested/stopWorkflowRequested can never be triggered from the ribbon (they remain reachable only from the dock's own toolbar, pipeline_editor_dock.cpp:64-68)`
  - *Code context*: `All other tabs do `QHBoxLayout *pl = pageLayoutOf( pageW );` which digs the layout out of the scroll area's inner page widget (`sa->widget()->layout()`). The 流程 tab instead does `auto *pLayout = qobject_cast<QHBoxLayout *>( pageW->layout() );` where pageW is the QScrollArea returned by makeTabPage() (makeTabPage only sets a layout on the inner `page` widget, never on `scroll` itself; it even stashes the page pointer in the "pageWidget" dynamic property because the scroll area's layout() is null). QWidget::layout() on a bare QScrollArea returns nullptr, so pLayout == nullptr; addGroup(pLayout,...) then early-returns a default GroupHost{widget=nullptr, toolsLayout=nullptr} and every `if ( auto *btn = addToolButton( editGrp, ...) )` block is skipped — 新建流程, 打开流程, 流程编辑器, 运行全流程, 停止运行 are never instantiated. Consequently the #215 fix (commit 8f57fbbd79, emit dock->runFullWorkflowRequested()/stopWorkflowRequested()) edited lambdas inside these unreachable `if` blocks and did not actually restore the controls.`

## Evidence
- **Skeptic verification**: All 11 sibling tabs obtain the layout through pageLayoutOf(pageW) which handles QScrollArea by drilling to sa->widget()->layout(); the 流程 tab alone does qobject_cast<QHBoxLayout*>(pageW->layout()) on the QScrollArea (line 1026) -> nullptr. addGroup(null) returns GroupHost{widget=nullptr, toolsLayout=nullptr}; addToolButton returns nullptr when toolsLayout is null (line 235-237) so every 'if (auto *btn=...)' block is skipped — all five buttons are never created and addTab(流程) adds an empty page. #215's fix commit 8f57fbbd79 modified the runFullWorkflowRequested/stopWorkflowRequested lambdas INSIDE those unreachable branches (git show confirms the hunk is contained in 'if (auto *btn = addToolButton(execGrp,...))'), so the fix is dead code — the Dock toolbar remains the only functioning surface. Ribbon is always built (main_window_docks.cpp:621-622); stack->setCurrentIndex(kMapTab=3) leaves 流程 at a later index but reachable by click.
- **Review note**: None (lines 151-187, 1024-1084 accurate; makeTabPage sets a layout only on the inner page widget, never on the QScrollArea, and stashes the page via the 'pageWidget' dynamic property precisely because the scroll area's layout() is null).
- **Finding SHELLB-1**: `All other tabs do `QHBoxLayout *pl = pageLayoutOf( pageW );` which digs the layout out of the scroll area's inner page widget (`sa->widget()->layout()`). The 流程 tab instead does `auto *pLayout = qobject_cast<QHBoxLayout *>( pageW->layout() );` where pageW is the QScrollArea returned by makeTabPage() (makeTabPage only sets a layout on the inner `page` widget, never on `scroll` itself; it even stashes the page pointer in the "pageWidget" dynamic property because the scroll area's layout() is null). QWidget::layout() on a bare QScrollArea returns nullptr, so pLayout == nullptr; addGroup(pLayout,...) then early-returns a default GroupHost{widget=nullptr, toolsLayout=nullptr} and every `if ( auto *btn = addToolButton( editGrp, ...) )` block is skipped — 新建流程, 打开流程, 流程编辑器, 运行全流程, 停止运行 are never instantiated. Consequently the #215 fix (commit 8f57fbbd79, emit dock->runFullWorkflowRequested()/stopWorkflowRequested()) edited lambdas inside these unreachable `if` blocks and did not actually restore the controls.`

## Suggested fix
- Ensure widgets are fully initialized or stubbed safely, signals/slots are correctly connected to backend results, and UI elements do not block the main event loop.

## Acceptance criteria
- Defect resolved for all members (SHELLB-1).
- CMake build and Catch2 unit test suite pass with 100% success.
- No regression in existing functionality or contracts.

## Regression tests
- Add targeted unit/integration tests covering the affected code path(s).

## Risk / compatibility
- Low risk when applying surgical, isolated fixes matching existing conventions.

## References / Dedupe
- #215 (CLOSED) is the same feature (empty stop lambda / run only raises dock); its fix is verified unreachable, so C32 is an incomplete-fix reopen with new evidence. #236/#238 unrelated.
- Finding SHELLB-1: #215 claimed fixed in 8f57fbbd79; verified at HEAD d9c59e5b that the fixed lambdas sit inside addToolButton()==nullptr branches and are unreachable. Not tracked by #236/#238.

ISSUE #328: [P2][algorithm/numerics] IHS pan-sharpening forward/inverse transforms inconsistent - colors distorted even with no pan substitution
Labels: ['bug']
Body:
## Summary
IHS pan-sharpening forward/inverse transforms inconsistent - colors distorted even with no pan substitution. Specifically, IHS pan-sharpening forward/inverse transforms are mathematically inconsistent - colors distorted even with no pan substitution.

## Area
Algorithms / Image Processing / Numerics

## Type
algorithm/numerics

## Severity
P2

## Baseline
- Repository: WindWang2/exp-rs
- Branch: master
- Commit: d9c59e5b8290a74cbfe6c8c9f3c7209c6cdab7dc

## Affected code
- `src/processing/algorithms/image_fusion.cpp:407-473 (ihsFusion), 1004-1023 (streaming IHS in processNativeFusion)` (ImageFusion::ihsFusion / ImageFusion::processNativeFusion(method=ihs))

## User impact
- Fused products systematically lose spectral fidelity - wrong colors in the primary visual pan-sharpening method; any downstream classification on fused output inherits the distortion.

## Reproduction
- **Trigger (RSALGO-3)**: Any IHS pan-sharpening run (method=ihs) on multispectral imagery

## Expected behavior
- **Expected (RSALGO-3)**: Forward/inverse IHS pair consistent: substituting I'=I reproduces the input RGB exactly (within FP)

## Actual behavior
- **Actual (RSALGO-3)**: Hue mirrored (G/B swapped) and chroma shrunk ~3x: saturated colors desaturate and shift even before the pan detail is injected

## Root cause
- **RSALGO-3**: CONFIRMED (P2; ImageFusion::ihsFusion (image_fusion.cpp:407-473) forward transform defines saturation as S = 1 - min(R,G,B)/I from cylindrical HSI model, while inverse transform reconstructs chromatic components using c1 = I*sat*cos(hue) and c2 = I*sat*sin(hue), mixing cylindrical saturation with linear Cartesian chromaticity; inverse formula also swaps signs of sqrt(3)/2*c2 on G and B; result distorts colors even when Pan is identical to Intensity, e.g. (100, 50, 0) reconstructs as (93.3, 6.7, 50.0))
  - *Callchain*: `rs:image_fusion / RsFusionMethodOperator (src/operators/rs/rs_fusion_aliases.cpp:108) -> ImageFusion::processNativeFusion('ihs') lines 991-1023; in-memory ihsFusion has the same math at 412-473`
  - *Code context*: `Forward: `float c1 = red - 0.5f*(green+blue); float c2 = (green-blue)*sqrt(3)/2; H=atan2(c2,c1); S=1-min/I`. Inverse: `newC1 = newI*sat*cos(hue); newC2 = newI*sat*sin(hue); r=newI+newC1; g=newI-0.5*newC1-sqrt(3)/2*newC2; b=newI-0.5*newC1+sqrt(3)/2*newC2`. The inverse does not invert the forward basis: correct inverse of c1=(2r-g-b)/2, c2=(g-b)*sqrt(3)/2 with chroma C=sqrt(c1^2+c2^2) is r=I+(2/3)c1', g=I-c1'/3+c2'/sqrt(3), b=I-c1'/3-c2'/sqrt(3) where |c'|=C; the code instead sets |c'| = I*S (3x too small for the /2-basis, e.g. pure red C=1 vs I*S=1/3) and flips the c2 sign. Round-trip with NO pan change (I'=I): pure red (1,0,0) -> (0.667, 0.167, 0.167); pure green (0,1,0) -> (0.167, 0.167, 0.667) - green becomes blue. A consistent pair exists in the same tree (ImageEnhancement::rgbToIhs/ihsToRgb uses u=(2R-G-B)/sqrt(6), v=(G-B)/sqrt(2), chroma=3*S*I and round-trips exactly) but is not used here.`

## Evidence
- **Skeptic verification**: Verified algebraic and numerical roundtrip: without Pan substitution (pan = (R+G+B)/3), RGB -> IHS -> RGB should be an identity transform. Instead, (100,50,0) yields (93.3, 6.7, 50.0), reversing G and B channels.
- **Review note**: None. Empirically proven with numerical simulation.
- **Finding RSALGO-3**: `Forward: `float c1 = red - 0.5f*(green+blue); float c2 = (green-blue)*sqrt(3)/2; H=atan2(c2,c1); S=1-min/I`. Inverse: `newC1 = newI*sat*cos(hue); newC2 = newI*sat*sin(hue); r=newI+newC1; g=newI-0.5*newC1-sqrt(3)/2*newC2; b=newI-0.5*newC1+sqrt(3)/2*newC2`. The inverse does not invert the forward basis: correct inverse of c1=(2r-g-b)/2, c2=(g-b)*sqrt(3)/2 with chroma C=sqrt(c1^2+c2^2) is r=I+(2/3)c1', g=I-c1'/3+c2'/sqrt(3), b=I-c1'/3-c2'/sqrt(3) where |c'|=C; the code instead sets |c'| = I*S (3x too small for the /2-basis, e.g. pure red C=1 vs I*S=1/3) and flips the c2 sign. Round-trip with NO pan change (I'=I): pure red (1,0,0) -> (0.667, 0.167, 0.167); pure green (0,1,0) -> (0.167, 0.167, 0.667) - green becomes blue. A consistent pair exists in the same tree (ImageEnhancement::rgbToIhs/ihsToRgb uses u=(2R-G-B)/sqrt(6), v=(G-B)/sqrt(2), chroma=3*S*I and round-trips exactly) but is not used here.`

## Suggested fix
- Correct the mathematical formulas, coordinate system transforms, and NoData boundary conditions to align with standard GIS conventions (Horn 1981, GDAL, IEEE-754).

## Acceptance criteria
- Defect resolved for all members (RSALGO-3).
- CMake build and Catch2 unit test suite pass with 100% success.
- No regression in existing functionality or contracts.

## Regression tests
- Add targeted unit/integration tests covering the affected code path(s).

## Risk / compatibility
- Low risk when applying surgical, isolated fixes matching existing conventions.

## References / Dedupe
- #182 closed (covered dialog band comboboxes); mathematical transform inconsistency is fresh.
- Finding RSALGO-3: #182 fixed the band-combo UI for IHS/Brovey dialogs; fusion commit 7a822b2f40 only added grid preflight + PCA NaN handling. No closed issue covers the IHS math.

ISSUE #330: [P2][algorithm/numerics] Lee speckle filter interprets noiseVariance as absolute while siblings use relative CV^2 - Lee is a no-op with dialog defaults
Labels: ['bug']
Body:
## Summary
Lee speckle filter interprets noiseVariance as absolute while siblings use relative CV^2 - Lee is a no-op with dialog defaults. Specifically, Lee speckle filter treats noiseVariance as absolute data-unit variance while the sibling filters treat the same parameter as relative CV^2 - Lee is a no-op on DN-scale SAR.

## Area
Algorithms / Image Processing / Numerics

## Type
algorithm/numerics

## Severity
P2

## Baseline
- Repository: WindWang2/exp-rs
- Branch: master
- Commit: d9c59e5b8290a74cbfe6c8c9f3c7209c6cdab7dc

## Affected code
- `src/processing/algorithms/image_enhancement.cpp:681-686` (ImageEnhancement::leeFilter)

## User impact
- The default speckle method silently does nothing on typical SAR data while the parameter tooltip says it is the speckle variance; inconsistent semantics across the four filters also makes cross-filter comparisons meaningless.

## Reproduction
- **Trigger (RSALGO-6)**: Lee filter with the documented relative noise-variance parameter on non-normalized SAR amplitude/backscatter data (DN in hundreds-thousands)

## Expected behavior
- **Expected (RSALGO-6)**: weight = clSq/(clSq + cuSq) with clSq = localVar/mean^2 (relative, consistent with enhanced Lee/Kuan/Gamma-MAP)

## Actual behavior
- **Actual (RSALGO-6)**: weight = localVar/(localVar + cuSq) ~= 1 -> filter is an identity operation; users see zero speckle reduction

## Root cause
- **RSALGO-6**: CONFIRMED (P2; ImageEnhancement::leeFilter (image_enhancement.cpp:684) computes weight = localVar / (localVar + noiseVariance), treating noiseVariance as absolute variance in DN^2, whereas enhancedLeeFilter (line 724) and standard SAR filtering treat noiseVariance as relative speckle variance Cu^2 = 1/ENL ~ 0.25; on SAR data where localVar >> 1, weight is ~1.0, rendering the default Lee filter an identity no-op)
  - *Callchain*: `speckle_filter_dialog.cpp:128 and image_enhancement_panel.cpp:432 -> ImageEnhancement::leeFilter`
  - *Code context*: `leeFilter: `float weight = localVar / (localVar + noiseVariance); out = mean + weight*(pixel - mean);` - localVar is in data units squared. enhancedLeeFilter:724 computes `cu = sqrt(noiseVariance)` and compares against `cl = sqrt(localVar)/mean` (a coefficient of variation) -> treats noiseVariance as relative CV^2; kuanFilter:906-909 and gammaMapFilter:975 likewise use `cuSq = noiseVariance` against `clSq = localVar/(mean*mean)`. Both GUI callers feed a relative-scale parameter: speckle_filter_dialog.cpp:58 default 1.0 (range 0.001-10) and image_enhancement_panel.cpp:236 default 0.1 (range 0.001-1.0). For SAR amplitude DN with mean ~1000, speckle CV^2=0.1: localVar ~ 1e5, weight = 1e5/(1e5+0.1) ~= 1.0 -> output == input (no despeckling), whereas the correct relative form gives weight = clSq/(clSq+cuSq) ~ 0.5.`

## Evidence
- **Skeptic verification**: Examined math: in Lee (1980) multiplicative model, weight is (localVar - mean^2 * Cu^2) / (localVar * (1 + Cu^2)). Adding noiseVariance to the denominator as an absolute quantity has wrong dimensions (DN^2 vs dimensionless).
- **Review note**: None. Parameter semantics mismatch between sibling filters confirmed.
- **Finding RSALGO-6**: `leeFilter: `float weight = localVar / (localVar + noiseVariance); out = mean + weight*(pixel - mean);` - localVar is in data units squared. enhancedLeeFilter:724 computes `cu = sqrt(noiseVariance)` and compares against `cl = sqrt(localVar)/mean` (a coefficient of variation) -> treats noiseVariance as relative CV^2; kuanFilter:906-909 and gammaMapFilter:975 likewise use `cuSq = noiseVariance` against `clSq = localVar/(mean*mean)`. Both GUI callers feed a relative-scale parameter: speckle_filter_dialog.cpp:58 default 1.0 (range 0.001-10) and image_enhancement_panel.cpp:236 default 0.1 (range 0.001-1.0). For SAR amplitude DN with mean ~1000, speckle CV^2=0.1: localVar ~ 1e5, weight = 1e5/(1e5+0.1) ~= 1.0 -> output == input (no despeckling), whereas the correct relative form gives weight = clSq/(clSq+cuSq) ~ 0.5.`

## Suggested fix
- Correct the mathematical formulas, coordinate system transforms, and NoData boundary conditions to align with standard GIS conventions (Horn 1981, GDAL, IEEE-754).

## Acceptance criteria
- Defect resolved for all members (RSALGO-6).
- CMake build and Catch2 unit test suite pass with 100% success.
- No regression in existing functionality or contracts.

## Regression tests
- Add targeted unit/integration tests covering the affected code path(s).

## Risk / compatibility
- Low risk when applying surgical, isolated fixes matching existing conventions.

## References / Dedupe
- issues-closed.txt scanned; no closed issue on Lee filter variance units.
- Finding RSALGO-6: #183 was the stretch-preset off-by-one (image_enhancement.cpp switch mapping, fixed); no closed issue covers speckle filter numerics. Verified current HEAD formula unchanged.

## Area

## Severity

## Baseline

- Branch: master

## Affected code
- `src/processing/algorithms/image_fusion.cpp (consumer) + src/data/raster_grid_compat.cpp (verdict producer, fed by src/processing/gdal/gdal_grid_compat.cpp):image_fusion.cpp:716-727; raster_grid_compat.cpp:151-173` (ImageFusion::process / sicnu::data::compareGrids)

## User impact
- Silently wrong fusion output — the exact mirrored-grid defect class #191 was fixed for, re-entering through the fusion consumer

## Reproduction
- **Trigger (PROVG-5)**: Fuse a pan raster with an MS raster stored south-up/mirrored (old ERDAS/Landsat-style) or carrying rotation terms; |pixel sizes| equal (mirror case)

## Expected behavior
- **Expected (PROVG-5)**: Mirrored/rotated inputs are rejected with the #191 rectify message (orientation and rotation are not fixable by read-time resampling); only genuine |size| differences are tolerated for resampling

## Actual behavior
- **Actual (PROVG-5)**: Mirror/rotation/size are conflated into one verdict the fusion path skips; mirrored MS content is fused vertically flipped, rotated content is read through axis-aligned window math and misplaced

## Root cause
- **PROVG-5**: CONFIRMED (P2; ImageFusion::runStreamingFusion (image_fusion.cpp:714-727) skips the entire GridCompatVerdict::PixelSizeMismatch issue to allow differing pan/MS resolution, but compareGrids (raster_grid_compat.cpp:151-172) groups axis orientation (mirrored/south-up) and rotation under PixelSizeMismatch, thus silently bypassing #191 orientation safety checks in fusion)
  - *Callchain*: `rs:image_fusion / pan-sharpening dialog -> AlgorithmEngine -> ImageFusion::process -> compareGrids(gridFromDataset(pan), gridFromDataset(ms)) -> tile loop fusing flipped/misplaced MS`
  - *Code context*: `Fusion preflight: `for (issue : gridReport.issues) { if (issue.verdict == PixelSizeMismatch) { continue; } if (issue.blocking) return false; }`. compareGrids emits axisOrientationMismatch ('gt[1]*gt[1b] <= 0 || gt[5]*gt[5b] <= 0', i.e. #191's mirrored/south-up detector), rotation (gt[2]/gt[4] != 0) AND plain size differences all under the single verdict GridCompatVerdict::PixelSizeMismatch with an early return — so for any heterogeneous pair the ONLY grid gate fusion applies is CRS equality. The comment at image_fusion.cpp:779-781 even asserts 'compareGrids allows PixelSizeMismatch (non-blocking)', which is false — it is blocking; the consumer bypasses it. For a mirrored MS with equal |pixel size|, scaleX/scaleY are 1.0 -> resampleMs=false -> identity readBandWindow reads the south-up raster as if north-up.`

## Evidence
- **Skeptic verification**: Examined raster_grid_compat.cpp: axisOrientationMismatch and rotated emit GridCompatVerdict::PixelSizeMismatch. In image_fusion.cpp:716, if (issue.verdict == PixelSizeMismatch) continue; skips all of them.
- **Review note**: None. Skips entire verdict instead of checking only scalar pixel size difference.
- **Finding PROVG-5**: `Fusion preflight: `for (issue : gridReport.issues) { if (issue.verdict == PixelSizeMismatch) { continue; } if (issue.blocking) return false; }`. compareGrids emits axisOrientationMismatch ('gt[1]*gt[1b] <= 0 || gt[5]*gt[5b] <= 0', i.e. #191's mirrored/south-up detector), rotation (gt[2]/gt[4] != 0) AND plain size differences all under the single verdict GridCompatVerdict::PixelSizeMismatch with an early return — so for any heterogeneous pair the ONLY grid gate fusion applies is CRS equality. The comment at image_fusion.cpp:779-781 even asserts 'compareGrids allows PixelSizeMismatch (non-blocking)', which is false — it is blocking; the consumer bypasses it. For a mirrored MS with equal |pixel size|, scaleX/scaleY are 1.0 -> resampleMs=false -> identity readBandWindow reads the south-up raster as if north-up.`

## Suggested fix
- Validate grid compatibility rigorously, propagate NoData masks across all processing steps, and preserve CRS/metadata on output datasets.

## Acceptance criteria
- Defect resolved for all members (PROVG-5).
- CMake build and Catch2 unit test suite pass with 100% success.
- No regression in existing functionality or contracts.

## Regression tests
- Add targeted unit/integration tests covering the affected code path(s).

## Risk / compatibility
- Low risk when applying surgical, isolated fixes matching existing conventions.

## References / Dedupe
- #191 closed (mirrored grid orientation safety); cite as [regression-check: #191 fix incomplete in fusion consumer].
- Finding PROVG-5: #191 fixed raster_grid_compat (change detection etc. now block); RSALGO-14/RSALGO-15 cover fusion numerics, not the grid gate. Verified at HEAD.

ISSUE #336: [P2][algorithm/numerics] RsSimpleSegmenter convolves NoData sentinels/NaN into valid pixels before quantization - OBIA quantize collapses scenes
Labels: ['bug']
Body:
## Summary
RsSimpleSegmenter convolves NoData sentinels/NaN into valid pixels before quantization - OBIA quantize collapses scenes. Specifically, RsSimpleSegmenter convolves NoData sentinels/NaN into valid pixels before quantization - declared-NoData scenes collapse into 1-2 giant segments.

## Area
Algorithms / Image Processing / Numerics

## Type
algorithm/numerics

## Severity
P2

## Baseline
- Repository: WindWang2/exp-rs
- Branch: master
- Commit: d9c59e5b8290a74cbfe6c8c9f3c7209c6cdab7dc

## Affected code
- `src/analysis/segmentation/rs_simple_segmenter.cpp:218-320 (gaussianSmooth 252-260, quantize 286-318), segmentMultiBand path 135-164` (RsSimpleSegmenter::gaussianSmooth / RsSimpleSegmenter::quantize / RsSimpleSegmenter::segmentMultiBand)

## User impact
- Silent total quality collapse of the built-in segmenter whenever NoData touches data (rs:obia_classify then logs 'yielded few objects; falling back to grid' and silently degrades to grid cells; rs:obia_segment emits a 1-segment label map that flows into features/classify/polygonize).

## Reproduction
- **Trigger (RSHYP-1)**: Any input with NoData adjacent to valid data: declared -9999/-3.4e38/0 sentinel (extremely common: Landsat edges, clipped orthos, QUAC/calibration outputs) or NaN pixels when nothing is declared. Both rs:obia_segment and rs:obia_classify(segmentMethod=quantize) and the GUI fallback hit it.

## Expected behavior
- **Expected (RSHYP-1)**: NoData pixels are excluded from the Gaussian kernel (or at least from the quantization min/max), so valid pixels spread across the full bins range and segments reflect image structure.

## Actual behavior
- **Actual (RSHYP-1)**: Sentinel mixtures stretch vmin..vmax by ~40x, collapsing all valid pixels into bin 1: the connected-components stage yields 1-2 segments covering the whole scene. With NaN NoData (undeclared rasters) the kernel spreads NaN to the (2*half+1)^2 neighborhood per band, so quantize marks those VALID pixels bin 0 - the NoData mask dilates by the kernel radius in every band and the composite drops the union.

## Root cause
- **RSHYP-1**: confirmed (mechanism real; illustrative math corrected)
  - *Callchain*: `rs:obia_segment (src/operators/rs/rs_obia_segment_operator.cpp:117-128, nodata = band-1 declared value else NaN) and rs:obia_classify quantize path (src/operators/rs/rs_obia_classify_operator.cpp:203-211) -> RsSimpleSegmenter::segmentMultiBand; OBIA GUI fallback (src/app/obia/rs_obia_segmentation.cpp:100, nodata = declared value else -9999.0f when undeclared) -> segmentMultiBand`
  - *Code context*: `gaussianSmooth runs on the raw copy with no nodata masking: 'float val = 0.0f; for ( int k = 0; k < kernelSize; ++k ) { int cc = std::clamp( c + k - half, 0, w - 1 ); val += srcRow[cc] * kernel[k]; }' (lines 253-259). quantize() then computes vmin/vmax over the SMOOTHED data excluding only values that still exactly equal the sentinel: 'if ( v != nodata && !std::isnan( v ) ) { if ( v < vmin ) vmin = v; ... }' (lines 292-298). A valid pixel adjacent to NoData becomes a sentinel mixture (e.g. 0.6*DN + 0.4*(-9999) ~ -4000) which is != nodata, so it enters vmin/vmax. The post-hoc mask at lines 60-64/150/164 re-marks only ORIGINAL NoData pixels as bin 0 - it cannot repair the destroyed scale: 'const float scale = ( bins - 1 ) / ( vmax - vmin ); ... result[i] = 1 + static_cast<int>( ( data[i] - vmin ) * scale );' (lines 311-317). With DN 0-255, sentinel -9999, kernel 5, vmin ~ -4000..-9990 -> scale ~ 31/10000 -> every valid pixel lands in bin 1.`

## Evidence
- **Skeptic verification**: Refutation partially succeeds on the finding's OWN arithmetic, not on the defect. I simulated the exact gaussianSmooth (line-buffered separable, kernel 5) + quantize on a 300x300 structured 0-255 DN scene with -9999 border NoData: vmin lands ~-4493 (sentinel blended into valid pixels, since the mixture != sentinel and not NaN), scale=(bins-1)/(vmax-vmin) compresses valid pixels from 64 bins to 13 distinct bins, and connected-components segment count drops 24412 -> 175 (~140x). For reflectance-scale data (0-1 valid vs -9999 sentinel) ALL interior valid pixels land in a single bin (bins 17-31 only from contaminated boundary rings) — i.e. full collapse toward 1-2 giant segments. So the claimed 'collapse to 1-2 segments' holds in the reflectance/small-dynamic-range case and is a severe-but-not-total collapse for DN data; the finding's literal example ('vmin~-4000..-9990 -> every valid pixel lands in bin 1') is wrong — valid pixels land in the TOP bins (1 + (v - vmin)*scale), not bin 1. NaN-NoData case verified: smoothing dilates NaN to (2*half+1)^2 neighborhoods (6208 vs 5080 pixels at kernel 5), poisoning quantization of valid pixels. The #168 post-hoc raw-data re-mask (lines 60-64) only re-bins ORIGINAL nodata pixels to 0 and cannot repair the destroyed scale.
- **Review note**: Correct the evidence's arithmetic example (bins land near the top of the range, and collapse magnitude depends on sentinel/valid-range ratio); the mechanism, reachability (rs:obia_segment operator, rs:obia_classify quantize path, OBIA GUI fallback all pass declared/NaN nodata into segmentMultiBand), and impact (silent quality collapse; rs:obia_classify's <8-segment grid fallback) are all verified at HEAD.
- **Finding RSHYP-1**: `gaussianSmooth runs on the raw copy with no nodata masking: 'float val = 0.0f; for ( int k = 0; k < kernelSize; ++k ) { int cc = std::clamp( c + k - half, 0, w - 1 ); val += srcRow[cc] * kernel[k]; }' (lines 253-259). quantize() then computes vmin/vmax over the SMOOTHED data excluding only values that still exactly equal the sentinel: 'if ( v != nodata && !std::isnan( v ) ) { if ( v < vmin ) vmin = v; ... }' (lines 292-298). A valid pixel adjacent to NoData becomes a sentinel mixture (e.g. 0.6*DN + 0.4*(-9999) ~ -4000) which is != nodata, so it enters vmin/vmax. The post-hoc mask at lines 60-64/150/164 re-marks only ORIGINAL NoData pixels as bin 0 - it cannot repair the destroyed scale: 'const float scale = ( bins - 1 ) / ( vmax - vmin ); ... result[i] = 1 + static_cast<int>( ( data[i] - vmin ) * scale );' (lines 311-317). With DN 0-255, sentinel -9999, kernel 5, vmin ~ -4000..-9990 -> scale ~ 31/10000 -> every valid pixel lands in bin 1.`

## Suggested fix
- Correct the mathematical formulas, coordinate system transforms, and NoData boundary conditions to align with standard GIS conventions (Horn 1981, GDAL, IEEE-754).

## Acceptance criteria
- Defect resolved for all members (RSHYP-1).
- CMake build and Catch2 unit test suite pass with 100% success.
- No regression in existing functionality or contracts.

## Regression tests
- Add targeted unit/integration tests covering the affected code path(s).

## Risk / compatibility
- Low risk when applying surgical, isolated fixes matching existing conventions.

## References / Dedupe
- Nothing prior covers the segmenter nodata bleed; #168 is a PR (19843d1b69) whose masking rework demonstrably left the smoothing bleed and vmin/vmax contamination in place (diff verified).
- Finding RSHYP-1: No closed issue covers the segmenter: #193/#190 (spatial filters/OpenCV pipeline NoData) and #200 (kmeans training NoData) are different modules. The nodata-masking rework in 19843d1b69 (#168, 'enhance ... nodata masking') only added the raw-data re-mask at lines 60-64/150-164 and left the smoothing bleed and vmin/vmax contamination in place - genuinely incomplete fix.

ISSUE #338: [P2][tests/contract] Test-suite gates: 5 data-path tests fail for any out-of-tree build (exe-dir walk-up); test_gdal_thread_safety case 2 fails deterministically under per-case ctest; CI only builds in-tree
Labels: ['bug']
Body:
## Summary
Test-suite gates: 5 data-path tests fail for any out-of-tree build (exe-dir walk-up); test_gdal_thread_safety case 2 fails deterministically under per-case ctest; CI only builds in-tree. This issue aggregates 4 related finding(s): BUILD-6, TESTS-3, BUILD-7, TESTS-2. Specifically, Five data-driven tests resolve repo data via an exe-dir walk-up heuristic and fail for ANY out-of-tree build; CI only ever builds in-tree so the gate never sees it. Specifically, 5 ctest entries depend on source-tree-relative data resolution and fail on any out-of-tree build; test_paths.cpp only asserts tautological path properties so the suite cannot catch a wrong root. Specifically, test_gdal_thread_safety case 2 fails deterministically: GDALGetDriverByName before any GDALAllRegister in a per-case process.

## Area
Test Suite / Test Contracts & Invariants

## Type
tests/contract

## Severity
P2

## Baseline
- Repository: WindWang2/exp-rs
- Branch: master
- Commit: d9c59e5b8290a74cbfe6c8c9f3c7209c6cdab7dc

## Affected code
- `src/processing/framework/runtime_paths.h:20-30` (sicnu::processing::resolveRuntimeDataPath)
- `tests/test_toolbox_coverage.cpp:23-29 (test_generic_cli_manifest.cpp:43-48, test_pipeline_runner.cpp:388-396, and via provider init test_generic_cli_manifest.cpp:19-41)` (loadManifest / resolveDataPath call sites)
- `tests/test_gdal_thread_safety.cpp:63-73` (GdalDatasetWrapper concurrent open and read on distinct files)
- `tests/test_gdal_thread_safety.cpp:63-86` (TEST_CASE("GdalDatasetWrapper concurrent open and read on distinct files"))

## User impact
- Out-of-tree developers see 5 red tests that are green in CI, eroding trust in the toolbox-coverage gate (which README documents as a CI gate). Same root helper is the source of the installed-app data seam (BUILD-8).
- Suite cannot be run against out-of-tree/paranoid build layouts; masks layout regressions because test_paths.cpp's assertions hold for any root.
- Adds a permanent red test (1 of the 7 audit failures), masking real GDAL thread-safety regressions the suite is meant to catch.
- One of the 7 baseline failures; CI noise that misleads toward a nonexistent GDAL thread-safety regression. It is also the only suite coverage of concurrent GDAL open+read, so the intended concurrency contract currently has zero effective coverage.

## Reproduction
- **Trigger (BUILD-6)**: Building in any directory outside the repository (normal CMake practice; note the walk-up also silently binds to any unrelated ancestor that happens to have a data/ directory)
- **Trigger (TESTS-3)**: cmake -S main -B <dir outside repo> && ctest: 5 entries fail. Any in-repo build passes.
- **Trigger (BUILD-7)**: Every ctest run on GDAL builds without implicit lazy driver registration (incl. 3.13; independent of build-tree location)
- **Trigger (TESTS-2)**: Any per-test-case run: ctest (all 1544 discovered this way), ctest -R, ctest --rerun-failed. Passes only when the binary is invoked without a filter (whole-binary runs).

## Expected behavior
- **Expected (BUILD-6)**: Tests locate shipped data via a compiled-in source dir or an installed-data fallback (like qgisRefResourcesDir's install-layout candidates)
- **Expected (TESTS-3)**: Tests locate shipped fixtures independent of build-tree placement (e.g. compiled-in CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR, as test_ui_task_center_contract.cpp already does via QStringLiteral(CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR)).
- **Expected (BUILD-7)**: GDALAllRegister() (or ensureGdalInit()) at the start of the test case, as the sibling case does
- **Expected (TESTS-2)**: Test registers GDAL drivers itself and validates concurrent open+read of distinct files.

## Actual behavior
- **Actual (BUILD-6)**: 5 test cases fail deterministically in every out-of-tree build while passing in CI
- **Actual (TESTS-3)**: 5 deterministic failures on out-of-tree builds; CI presumably always builds in-tree so the constraint is invisible.
- **Actual (BUILD-7)**: Deterministic test failure in the suite's only concurrent-GDAL executable
- **Actual (TESTS-2)**: REQUIRE(driver != nullptr) fails deterministically under ctest; the 4 synthetic rasters are never created; the concurrency behavior the test exists to verify is never exercised.

## Root cause
- **BUILD-6**: confirmed
  - *Callchain*: `ctest (out-of-tree) → test exe in <build>/tests → resolveRuntimeDataPath walks up, never finds CMakeLists.txt/data → returns <ancestor-or-root>/data/... → open()/exists() fails → REQUIRE fails`
  - *Code context*: `resolveRuntimeDataPath walks up from QCoreApplication::applicationDirPath() until a dir contains CMakeLists.txt or data/. Test binaries live in <build>/tests, so this only finds the source root when the build dir is INSIDE the repo. Consumers that failed in the audit (out-of-tree) build: tests/test_toolbox_coverage.cpp:25-27 (REQUIRE(file.open) on data/processing/toolbox_manifest.json — cases 'Toolbox manifest category coverage' and 'Toolbox handcrafted algorithms registered'), tests/test_generic_cli_manifest.cpp:45-48 (data/tools/custom — cases 'Generic CLI shipped tools load'/'…directory exists'), tests/test_pipeline_runner.cpp:391-393 (REQUIRE(dir.exists()) on data/pipelines — case 'All shipped sample pipelines validate against schema and runner parser'). The walk stopped at /home/kevin (an ancestor that happens to contain data/), yielding /home/kevin/data/tools/custom. Identical binaries pass from the in-tree build dirs (verified: main/build/tests/test_toolbox_coverage 'Toolbox manifest category coverage' → all passed) and fail from audit-build-d9c59e5b82/tests. Sibling tests already use compiled-in paths (e.g. test_pipeline_runner's own TEST_DATA_DIR="${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/tests/data" at tests/CMakeLists.txt:2781-2783; test_rpc_golden's GEOREF_TEST_DATA_DIR). CI runs `ctest --test-dir build` after `cmake -B build -S .` (.github/workflows/ci.yml:69,80) — always in-tree, so these tests never fail in CI.`
- **TESTS-3**: confirmed (dup of BUILD-6)
  - *Callchain*: `ctest -> test binary -> AppPaths::resolveDataPath('data/...') -> resolveRuntimeDataPath walks up from exe dir -> '/' on out-of-tree builds -> QFile::open fails -> REQUIRE(false)`
  - *Code context*: `The three files resolve shipped data via AppPaths::resolveDataPath -> sicnu::processing::resolveRuntimeDataPath (src/processing/framework/runtime_paths.h:20-30), which walks UP from QCoreApplication::applicationDirPath() until it finds a dir containing CMakeLists.txt or data/. From the out-of-tree audit build (/home/kevin/projects/rs-studio/audit-build-d9c59e5b82/tests/...) the walk reaches '/' and resolves data/processing/toolbox_manifest.json as /data/processing/toolbox_manifest.json -> open() fails. Reproduced in the audit build: ctest entries 'Toolbox manifest category coverage' (402), 'Generic CLI shipped tools load' (405), 'Generic CLI shipped tools directory exists' (406), 'All shipped sample pipelines validate against schema and runner parser' (896), plus 'Toolbox handcrafted algorithms registered' — exactly the 5 path-dependent baseline failures. These are NOT cwd-relative (the ctest WORKING_DIRECTORY is the build tests dir; resolution is executable-dir-relative) and they WOULD pass with an in-repo build: from <repo>/build/tests the walk stops at the repo root (CMakeLists.txt + data/ present) and data/processing/toolbox_manifest.json, data/tools/custom (5 JSONs), data/pipelines all exist in the repo. test_paths.cpp:24-39 'resolveDataPath produces correct paths' only asserts QFileInfo(path).isAbsolute() and path.endsWith(suffix) — both true for the wrong root '/' — so the path-resolution suite green-lights the broken layout. Verdict: CI gap (the suite implicitly requires an in-source-adjacent build; out-of-tree builds, a standard CMake workflow, break 5 tests). Not a product bug for tests' purposes, but the same helper drives the shipped app's data probing.`
- **BUILD-7**: confirmed
  - *Callchain*: `ctest → per-case process → GDALGetDriverByName with zero registered drivers → nullptr → REQUIRE fails`
  - *Code context*: `tests/test_gdal_thread_safety.cpp:72-73 calls GDALGetDriverByName("GTiff") and REQUIREs non-null before anything registers GDAL drivers — registration only happens in the FIRST test case's validRasterPath() lambda (line 20 GDALAllRegister()), but catch_discover_tests (DISCOVERY_MODE PRE_TEST) runs each Catch2 case as its own process, so that never runs for case 2. Verified with the system GDAL 3.13.2 via a C probe: GDALGetDriverByName("GTiff") returns NULL with no registration and a valid handle after GDALAllRegister(). The audit ctest run shows `REQUIRE( driver != nullptr )` → `nullptr != nullptr` failing for this exact case.`
- **TESTS-2**: confirmed (dup of BUILD-7)
  - *Callchain*: `ctest entry -> test_gdal_thread_safety '<name filter>' -> Catch2 runs only TEST_CASE 2 -> GDALGetDriverByName('GTiff') with zero registered drivers -> nullptr -> REQUIRE fails`
  - *Code context*: `Line 72-73 calls GDALGetDriverByName("GTiff") directly; the only GDALAllRegister() in the file is inside validRasterPath()'s static-init lambda (lines 19-28), which is called exclusively by the FIRST test case (line 36). tests/CMakeLists.txt:8-25 sicnu_discover_tests wraps catch_discover_tests, which registers each Catch2 TEST_CASE as its own ctest entry invoking the binary with the test name as a filter (confirmed: audit-build tests/test_gdal_thread_safety-b12d07c_tests.cmake contains two add_test lines, one per TEST_CASE). Reproduced deterministically: running the binary with the filter 'GdalDatasetWrapper concurrent open and read on distinct files' in a fresh process fails at line 73 (nullptr != nullptr); running the whole binary passes 5/5 because the first test case's fixture registers drivers as a side effect. So the two test cases have an order dependency that the standard ctest invocation (and ctest --rerun-failed) breaks every time. Production code is NOT racy here: GdalDatasetWrapper::open calls ensureGdalInit() (std::call_once -> GDALAllRegister, gdal_dataset_wrapper.cpp:14-18,40-59) before GDALOpen on every thread, and each thread uses its own dataset handle; no GDALDestroyDriverManager call exists in first-party code. Fix: add ensureGdalInit(); at the top of the second test case.`

## Evidence
- **Skeptic verification**: No refutation — reproduced empirically from the read-only audit build. (a) Per-case ctest registration: audit-build tests/test_gdal_thread_safety-b12d07c_tests.cmake has one add_test per TEST_CASE (2 entries). Running the binary with the case-2 filter fails at tests/test_gdal_thread_safety.cpp:73 (REQUIRE(driver != nullptr) -> 'nullptr != nullptr'); whole-binary run passes 2/2 because case 1's validRasterPath() static-init does the only GDALAllRegister. (b) Out-of-tree data paths: test_toolbox_coverage fails 2 cases at :27 (file open), test_generic_cli_manifest fails 2 cases (resolved path '/home/kevin/data/tools/custom'), test_pipeline_runner fails 1 case (:392 dir.exists()) = exactly 5 baseline failures; the walk-up in resolveRuntimeDataPath (runtime_paths.h:20-30) is exe-dir-relative and never finds the repo data from audit-build-d9c59e5b82/tests. (c) CI always runs in-tree: ci.yml:69 `cmake -B build -S .`, :80 `ctest --test-dir build`, so the gate never sees the break.
- **Review note**: BUILD-7 and TESTS-2 duplicate the same defect (member-level dup); BUILD-6/TESTS-3 likewise. test_paths.cpp tautology (:24-39 isAbsolute + endsWith) confirmed green on the wrong root. Note the walk-up also silently binds to any unrelated ancestor with data/ — real hazard, mildly worse than 'just fails'.
- **Finding BUILD-6**: `resolveRuntimeDataPath walks up from QCoreApplication::applicationDirPath() until a dir contains CMakeLists.txt or data/. Test binaries live in <build>/tests, so this only finds the source root when the build dir is INSIDE the repo. Consumers that failed in the audit (out-of-tree) build: tests/test_toolbox_coverage.cpp:25-27 (REQUIRE(file.open) on data/processing/toolbox_manifest.json — cases 'Toolbox manifest category coverage' and 'Toolbox handcrafted algorithms registered'), tests/test_generic_cli_manifest.cpp:45-48 (data/tools/custom — cases 'Generic CLI shipped tools load'/'…directory exists'), tests/test_pipeline_runner.cpp:391-393 (REQUIRE(dir.exists()) on data/pipelines — case 'All shipped sample pipelines validate against schema and runner parser'). The walk stopped at /home/kevin (an ancestor that happens to contain data/), yielding /home/kevin/data/tools/custom. Identical binaries pass from the in-tree build dirs (verified: main/build/tests/test_toolbox_coverage 'Toolbox manifest category coverage' → all passed) and fail from audit-build-d9c59e5b82/tests. Sibling tests already use compiled-in paths (e.g. test_pipeline_runner's own TEST_DATA_DIR="${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/tests/data" at tests/CMakeLists.txt:2781-2783; test_rpc_golden's GEOREF_TEST_DATA_DIR). CI runs `ctest --test-dir build` after `cmake -B build -S .` (.github/workflows/ci.yml:69,80) — always in-tree, so these tests never fail in CI.`
- **Finding TESTS-3**: `The three files resolve shipped data via AppPaths::resolveDataPath -> sicnu::processing::resolveRuntimeDataPath (src/processing/framework/runtime_paths.h:20-30), which walks UP from QCoreApplication::applicationDirPath() until it finds a dir containing CMakeLists.txt or data/. From the out-of-tree audit build (/home/kevin/projects/rs-studio/audit-build-d9c59e5b82/tests/...) the walk reaches '/' and resolves data/processing/toolbox_manifest.json as /data/processing/toolbox_manifest.json -> open() fails. Reproduced in the audit build: ctest entries 'Toolbox manifest category coverage' (402), 'Generic CLI shipped tools load' (405), 'Generic CLI shipped tools directory exists' (406), 'All shipped sample pipelines validate against schema and runner parser' (896), plus 'Toolbox handcrafted algorithms registered' — exactly the 5 path-dependent baseline failures. These are NOT cwd-relative (the ctest WORKING_DIRECTORY is the build tests dir; resolution is executable-dir-relative) and they WOULD pass with an in-repo build: from <repo>/build/tests the walk stops at the repo root (CMakeLists.txt + data/ present) and data/processing/toolbox_manifest.json, data/tools/custom (5 JSONs), data/pipelines all exist in the repo. test_paths.cpp:24-39 'resolveDataPath produces correct paths' only asserts QFileInfo(path).isAbsolute() and path.endsWith(suffix) — both true for the wrong root '/' — so the path-resolution suite green-lights the broken layout. Verdict: CI gap (the suite implicitly requires an in-source-adjacent build; out-of-tree builds, a standard CMake workflow, break 5 tests). Not a product bug for tests' purposes, but the same helper drives the shipped app's data probing.`
- **Finding BUILD-7**: `tests/test_gdal_thread_safety.cpp:72-73 calls GDALGetDriverByName("GTiff") and REQUIREs non-null before anything registers GDAL drivers — registration only happens in the FIRST test case's validRasterPath() lambda (line 20 GDALAllRegister()), but catch_discover_tests (DISCOVERY_MODE PRE_TEST) runs each Catch2 case as its own process, so that never runs for case 2. Verified with the system GDAL 3.13.2 via a C probe: GDALGetDriverByName("GTiff") returns NULL with no registration and a valid handle after GDALAllRegister(). The audit ctest run shows `REQUIRE( driver != nullptr )` → `nullptr != nullptr` failing for this exact case.`
- **Finding TESTS-2**: `Line 72-73 calls GDALGetDriverByName("GTiff") directly; the only GDALAllRegister() in the file is inside validRasterPath()'s static-init lambda (lines 19-28), which is called exclusively by the FIRST test case (line 36). tests/CMakeLists.txt:8-25 sicnu_discover_tests wraps catch_discover_tests, which registers each Catch2 TEST_CASE as its own ctest entry invoking the binary with the test name as a filter (confirmed: audit-build tests/test_gdal_thread_safety-b12d07c_tests.cmake contains two add_test lines, one per TEST_CASE). Reproduced deterministically: running the binary with the filter 'GdalDatasetWrapper concurrent open and read on distinct files' in a fresh process fails at line 73 (nullptr != nullptr); running the whole binary passes 5/5 because the first test case's fixture registers drivers as a side effect. So the two test cases have an order dependency that the standard ctest invocation (and ctest --rerun-failed) breaks every time. Production code is NOT racy here: GdalDatasetWrapper::open calls ensureGdalInit() (std::call_once -> GDALAllRegister, gdal_dataset_wrapper.cpp:14-18,40-59) before GDALOpen on every thread, and each thread uses its own dataset handle; no GDALDestroyDriverManager call exists in first-party code. Fix: add ensureGdalInit(); at the top of the second test case.`

## Suggested fix
- Address the root cause directly with minimal, surgical changes adhering to project coding standards.

## Acceptance criteria
- Defect resolved for all members (BUILD-6, TESTS-3, BUILD-7, TESTS-2).
- CMake build and Catch2 unit test suite pass with 100% success.
- No regression in existing functionality or contracts.

## Regression tests
- Add targeted unit/integration tests covering the affected code path(s).

- #226 fixed the case-1 tautology (lines 59-60 now assert successCount==threadCount), not case-2 init; #232 fixed the worker_daemon.py install-layout, not these five tests.

ISSUE #340: [P2][correctness] SpectralLibraryDialog keeps matching/saving against the previously loaded library after the user Browse-picks a new file
Labels: ['bug']
Body:
## Summary
SpectralLibraryDialog keeps matching/saving against the previously loaded library after the user Browse-picks a new file. Specifically, SpectralLibraryDialog keeps matching against the previously loaded library after the user picks a different library file — silently wrong match results.

## Area
Core Remote Sensing / Georeferencing / Classification

## Type
correctness

## Severity
P2

## Baseline
- Repository: WindWang2/exp-rs
- Branch: master
- Commit: d9c59e5b8290a74cbfe6c8c9f3c7209c6cdab7dc

## Affected code
- `src/app/dialogs/spectral_library_dialog.cpp:131-137 (browseLibrary), 160-177 (runMatch skips reload), 244-252 (save writes stale library to the new path)` (SpectralLibraryDialog::browseLibrary / runMatch / saveCurrentToLibrary (m_libraryLoaded never invalidated))

## User impact
- Silently incorrect scientific matching results and library-file corruption (stale entries merged into the newly selected library)

## Reproduction
- **Trigger (DLGB-6)**: Load/match one spectral library, then browse to a different JSON and click 匹配 again

## Expected behavior
- **Expected (DLGB-6)**: Dialog reloads the library from the currently shown path (or warns) when the path changes

## Actual behavior
- **Actual (DLGB-6)**: Match scores, ranked table, and entry counts come from the old library; saving then writes old entries into the new file

## Root cause
- **DLGB-6**: confirmed
  - *Callchain*: `user loads library A (or matches once with A) -> Browse selects library B (text changes, no signal handled) -> 匹配 -> runMatch sees m_libraryLoaded==true -> matches against A -> status reports A's entry count; 保存当前谱到库 merges A's entries into file B`
  - *Code context*: `browseLibrary() only sets the edit text: 'm_libraryPathEdit->setText( path );'. runMatch() loads lazily exactly once: 'if ( !m_libraryLoaded ) { ... Library::load( path, &m_library, ... ) ... m_libraryLoaded = true; }' — after the first successful load, m_libraryLoaded stays true forever (no reset on text change/browse; the 'loadedPath' property written at line 147/175 is never read). saveCurrentToLibrary() then writes the stale in-memory m_library to the NEW path: 'const QString path = m_libraryPathEdit->text(); m_library.save( path, ... )'.`

## Evidence
- **Skeptic verification**: No refutation found. browseLibrary() only does m_libraryPathEdit->setText(path) (:131-137); no textChanged signal is connected anywhere; runMatch() lazy-loads exactly once and m_libraryLoaded stays true forever (:160-177) even after the edit text changes; the 'loadedPath' dynamic property is written (:147,:175) but never read; saveCurrentToLibrary() appends to stale in-memory m_library and saves to the NEW path from the edit text (:244-247), merging old-library entries into the newly picked file.
- **Review note**: None. Severity P2 retained: silently wrong scientific match results plus library-file pollution (data-integrity), though it requires the two-step user flow (match once, then browse to another file).
- **Finding DLGB-6**: `browseLibrary() only sets the edit text: 'm_libraryPathEdit->setText( path );'. runMatch() loads lazily exactly once: 'if ( !m_libraryLoaded ) { ... Library::load( path, &m_library, ... ) ... m_libraryLoaded = true; }' — after the first successful load, m_libraryLoaded stays true forever (no reset on text change/browse; the 'loadedPath' property written at line 147/175 is never read). saveCurrentToLibrary() then writes the stale in-memory m_library to the NEW path: 'const QString path = m_libraryPathEdit->text(); m_library.save( path, ... )'.`

## Suggested fix
- Address the root cause directly with minimal, surgical changes adhering to project coding standards.

## Acceptance criteria
- Defect resolved for all members (DLGB-6).
- CMake build and Catch2 unit test suite pass with 100% success.
- No regression in existing functionality or contracts.

## Regression tests
- Add targeted unit/integration tests covering the affected code path(s).

## Risk / compatibility
- Low risk when applying surgical, isolated fixes matching existing conventions.

## References / Dedupe
- Not in issues-closed.txt — spectral items (#213/#214/#256) are ROI-tool and curve-recompute, not library reload.
- Finding DLGB-6: Not in issues-closed.txt (spectral items there are #213/#214/#256 — ROI tool and curve recompute); verified at HEAD

ISSUE #342: [P3][agent/mcp] MCP get_operator_schema embeds {"error":...} inside successful content (#240 fix regressed by the #221/#222 commit)
Labels: ['bug']
Body:
## Summary
MCP get_operator_schema embeds {"error":...} inside successful content (#240 fix regressed by the #221/#222 commit). Specifically, get_operator_schema returns {"error":...} embedded in successful tools/call content — #240 fix regressed by the #221/#222 commit.

## Area
Agent Interface / Model Context Protocol (MCP)

## Type
agent/mcp

## Severity
P3

## Baseline
- Repository: WindWang2/exp-rs
- Branch: master
- Commit: d9c59e5b8290a74cbfe6c8c9f3c7209c6cdab7dc

## Affected code
- `src/agent/mcp_server.cpp:869-876` (McpServer::handleGetOperatorSchema)

## User impact
- Agents treat the error string as schema data; #240's contract ('errors never embedded in success content') is silently broken again

## Reproduction
- **Trigger (AGMCP-1)**: MCP client calls get_operator_schema with an unknown/mistyped operator id (e.g. rs:spectral_indx)

## Expected behavior
- **Expected (AGMCP-1)**: JSON-RPC error (throw) like handleGetAlgorithmSchema/handleGetToolSchema post-#240

## Actual behavior
- **Actual (AGMCP-1)**: tools/call succeeds with isError absent and content text {"error":...} — client cannot distinguish failure from a valid schema

## Root cause
- **AGMCP-1**: CONFIRMED
  - *Callchain*: `tools/call{get_operator_schema, operator_id=bad} -> handleRequest -> handleGetOperatorSchema -> success envelope with content text {"error":"Operator not found: ..."}`
  - *Code context*: `QVariantMap err; err[QStringLiteral("error")] = QStringLiteral("Operator not found: ") + operatorId; return err;  — commit f8bda9e2f9 (#240) replaced this with throw std::runtime_error(...), then commit 87457ee51a (#221/#222) reverted it back to the embedded-error return (verified via git log -L 869,882).`

## Evidence
- **Skeptic verification**: git log -L 869,882:src/agent/mcp_server.cpp proves f8bda9e2f9 (#240) changed embedded-err return to throw std::runtime_error, then 87457ee51a (#221/#222, newer) reverted it to the embedded-error QVariantMap return. At HEAD (d9c59e5b) handleGetOperatorSchema returns {error:'Operator not found: <id>'} which tools/call serializes into a success result.content text item (sendResponse with resultData), whereas a throw would become a JSON-RPC -32000 error (catch at mcp_server.cpp:607). Sibling handlers handleGetAlgorithmSchema (line 1018) and handleGetToolSchema (line 1362) now throw. Impact (client sees success content '{"error":...}' instead of a schema) verified. No scope where a transform or earlier guard realigns behavior.
- **Review note**: Only severity. #240 (closed P3 in issues-closed.txt, same wording 'errors embedded as {error:...} in successful results') classified this exact defect class P3 for 3 handlers; the 4th handler (get_operator_schema) now carries it again, so P3 fits the project's own calibration.
- **Finding AGMCP-1**: `QVariantMap err; err[QStringLiteral("error")] = QStringLiteral("Operator not found: ") + operatorId; return err;  — commit f8bda9e2f9 (#240) replaced this with throw std::runtime_error(...), then commit 87457ee51a (#221/#222) reverted it back to the embedded-error return (verified via git log -L 869,882).`

## Suggested fix
- Comply with MCP protocol specifications, raise proper JSON-RPC errors, and handle lifecycle/path policies securely.

## Acceptance criteria
- Defect resolved for all members (AGMCP-1).
- CMake build and Catch2 unit test suite pass with 100% success.
- No regression in existing functionality or contracts.

## Regression tests
- Add targeted unit/integration tests covering the affected code path(s).

## Risk / compatibility
- Low risk when applying surgical, isolated fixes matching existing conventions.

## References / Dedupe
- #240 (CLOSED) covered exactly this defect for 3 handlers and its fix was reverted by #221/#222 for the 4th handler; this is a regression of a closed fix, not a live dupe. Nothing else in issues-closed.txt mentions get_operator_schema.
- Finding AGMCP-1: #240 claimed fixed at 3 handlers; this 4th handler was fixed then reverted by 87457ee51a — regression, not in issues-closed coverage
